### Example 4: Controller Endpoint

csharp

```
// CMR.Modules.Sales.Api/Controllers/SalesOrdersController.cs

[ApiController]
[Route("api/v1/sales/orders")]
[Authorize]
[Tags("Sales")]
public sealed class SalesOrdersController : ControllerBase
{
    private readonly ISender _sender;      // MediatR

    public SalesOrdersController(ISender sender)
    {
        _sender = sender;
    }

    /// <summary>List sales orders with pagination and filters</summary>
    [HttpGet]
    [Authorize(Policy = "Sales.Order.View")]
    [ProducesResponseType(typeof(PagedResult<SalesOrderListItem>), 200)]
    public async Task<IActionResult> GetAll(
        [FromQuery] GetSalesOrdersQuery query,
        CancellationToken cancellationToken)
    {
        var result = await _sender.Send(query, cancellationToken);
        return Ok(ApiResponse.Success(result));
    }

    /// <summary>Create a new sales order (draft)</summary>
    [HttpPost]
    [Authorize(Policy = "Sales.Order.Create")]
    [ProducesResponseType(typeof(SalesOrderResponse), 201)]
    [ProducesResponseType(typeof(ProblemDetails), 422)]
    public async Task<IActionResult> Create(
        [FromBody] CreateSalesOrderRequest request,
        CancellationToken cancellationToken)
    {
        var command = new CreateSalesOrderCommand
        {
            CustomerId = request.CustomerId,
            WarehouseId = request.WarehouseId,
            DeliveryDate = request.DeliveryDate,
            Notes = request.Notes,
            Items = request.Items.Select(i => new OrderItemDto
            {
                ProductId = i.ProductId,
                Quantity = i.Quantity,
                UnitPrice = i.UnitPrice,
                DiscountPct = i.DiscountPct
            }).ToList()
        };

        var result = await _sender.Send(command, cancellationToken);

        return CreatedAtAction(
            nameof(GetById),
            new { id = result.Id },
            ApiResponse.Success(result));
    }

    /// <summary>Get sales order by ID</summary>
    [HttpGet("{id:guid}")]
    [Authorize(Policy = "Sales.Order.View")]
    [ProducesResponseType(typeof(SalesOrderResponse), 200)]
    [ProducesResponseType(typeof(ProblemDetails), 404)]
    public async Task<IActionResult> GetById(
        Guid id,
        CancellationToken cancellationToken)
    {
        var result = await _sender.Send(
            new GetSalesOrderByIdQuery(id), cancellationToken);

        return result is null
            ? NotFound(ProblemDetailsFactory.CreateNotFound(id))
            : Ok(ApiResponse.Success(result));
    }

    /// <summary>Confirm a draft order</summary>
    [HttpPost("{id:guid}/confirm")]
    [Authorize(Policy = "Sales.Order.Confirm")]
    [ProducesResponseType(204)]
    [ProducesResponseType(typeof(ProblemDetails), 409)]
    public async Task<IActionResult> Confirm(
        Guid id,
        CancellationToken cancellationToken)
    {
        await _sender.Send(
            new ConfirmSalesOrderCommand(id), cancellationToken);

        return NoContent();
    }

    /// <summary>Cancel an order</summary>
    [HttpPost("{id:guid}/cancel")]
    [Authorize(Policy = "Sales.Order.Cancel")]
    [ProducesResponseType(204)]
    public async Task<IActionResult> Cancel(
        Guid id,
        [FromBody] CancelSalesOrderRequest request,
        CancellationToken cancellationToken)
    {
        await _sender.Send(
            new CancelSalesOrderCommand(id, request.Reason),
            cancellationToken);

        return NoContent();
    }
}
```

### Example 5: Module Communication (Domain Events via Outbox)

csharp

```
// When a SalesOrder is Confirmed → Inventory must reserve stock

// Step 1: Domain Event (in Sales.Domain)
public sealed record SalesOrderConfirmedEvent(
    Guid OrderId,
    Guid CustomerId,
    IReadOnlyList<OrderItemReservation> Items,
    Money TotalAmount) : IDomainEvent;

// Step 2: Event Handler in Sales.Application
// Converts domain event → outbox message (cross-module)
public class SalesOrderConfirmedEventHandler
    : IDomainEventHandler<SalesOrderConfirmedEvent>
{
    private readonly IOutboxService _outbox;

    public async Task Handle(
        SalesOrderConfirmedEvent notification,
        CancellationToken cancellationToken)
    {
        // Publish to outbox (not directly to Inventory module!)
        var integrationEvent = new StockReservationRequestedEvent(
            notification.OrderId,
            notification.Items.Select(i => new StockReservationItem(
                i.ProductId, i.WarehouseId, i.Quantity)).ToList());

        await _outbox.PublishAsync(integrationEvent, cancellationToken);
    }
}

// Step 3: Outbox Processor (Hangfire job, every 10 seconds)
public class OutboxProcessor : IRecurringJob
{
    public async Task ExecuteAsync()
    {
        // Read pending outbox messages
        // Dispatch to registered handlers by message type
        // Mark as sent / handle retries
    }
}

// Step 4: Inventory module subscribes to the event
// CMR.Modules.Inventory.Application/.../StockReservationHandler.cs
public class StockReservationRequestedHandler
    : IIntegrationEventHandler<StockReservationRequestedEvent>
{
    // Inventory module processes this independently
    // No direct code reference to Sales module!
}
```

### Example 6: Program.cs Wiring

csharp

```
// src/Api/Program.cs

var builder = WebApplication.CreateBuilder(args);

// -- Infrastructure --
builder.Services
    .AddPostgreSQL(builder.Configuration)       // DbContext + Dapper
    .AddIdentityAndJwt(builder.Configuration)   // ASP.NET Identity + JWT
    .AddHangfire(builder.Configuration)         // Background jobs
    .AddRedisCache(builder.Configuration)       // Caching
    .AddHttpContextAccessor()
    .AddMediatR(cfg => cfg.RegisterServicesFromAssemblies(
        typeof(CreateSalesOrderHandler).Assembly,
        typeof(CreateStockEntryHandler).Assembly
        // ... all module application assemblies
    ))
    .AddValidatorsFromAssemblies([...])
    .AddFluentValidation();

// -- Module registrations --
builder.Services
    .AddSalesModule(builder.Configuration)
    .AddInventoryModule(builder.Configuration)
    .AddFinanceModule(builder.Configuration)
    .AddPurchaseModule(builder.Configuration)
    .AddHRModule(builder.Configuration)
    .AddCRMModule(builder.Configuration)
    .AddIAMModule(builder.Configuration);

// -- Cross-cutting --
builder.Services
    .AddPipelineBehaviors()   // Validation + Logging + Transaction + Audit
    .AddCors(builder.Configuration)
    .AddRateLimiting()
    .AddApiVersioning()
    .AddSwagger();

var app = builder.Build();

// -- Middleware pipeline (ORDER MATTERS!) --
app.UseCorrelationId();
app.UseRequestLogging();
app.UseExceptionHandling();
app.UseHttpsRedirection();
app.UseCors();
app.UseRateLimiter();
app.UseAuthentication();
app.UseAuthorization();
app.UseBranchContext();        // Sets current branch from JWT claim
app.MapControllers();
app.UseHangfireDashboard("/jobs");
app.UseSwagger();

// -- Scheduled jobs --
RecurringJob.AddOrUpdate<StockReorderJob>(
    "stock-reorder-sweep",
    job => job.ExecuteAsync(),
    Cron.Daily(2));             // 2AM daily

RecurringJob.AddOrUpdate<PayrollRunJob>(
    "monthly-payroll",
    job => job.ExecuteAsync(),
    "0 2 28 * *");              // 28th every month

RecurringJob.AddOrUpdate<BankReconciliationJob>(
    "bank-recon",
    job => job.ExecuteAsync(),
    Cron.Daily(6));             // 6AM daily

RecurringJob.AddOrUpdate<OutboxProcessorJob>(
    "outbox-processor",
    job => job.ExecuteAsync(),
    "*/10 * * * * *");          // Every 10 seconds

app.Run();
```

---

# Non-Functional Requirements

## NFR Summary Table

text

```
┌────────────────┬────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ NFR Category   │ Specification                                  │
├────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Performance    │ • API p95 < 200ms (reads), < 500ms (writes)   │
│                │ • Report generation < 5s                       │
│                │ • DB queries < 50ms (indexed paths)            │
│                │ • Support 300 concurrent users                 │
├────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Availability   │ • 99.5% uptime SLA                            │
│                │ • Max 4 hours downtime/month                   │
│                │ • Auto health checks + restart (K8s/ECS)       │
├────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Security       │ • OWASP Top 10 compliance                      │
│                │ • TLS 1.3 in transit                           │
│                │ • AES-256 at rest (PAN, phone, bank no.)       │
│                │ • Rate limiting (100 req/min per user)         │
│                │ • Input validation on all endpoints            │
│                │ • SQL injection: parameterized queries only     │
│                │ • JWT: RS256 signing, 15-min access token      │
│                │ • Refresh tokens: rotated + revocable          │
├────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Compliance     │ • GST: e-invoice IRN generation via NIC API   │
│                │ • TDS: Form 16, 26Q automated                  │
│                │ • PF/ESI: challan generation                   │
│                │ • Audit trail: 7 years retention               │
│                │ • Data residency: India (Mumbai region)        │
├────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Scalability    │ Phase 1: Modular monolith (current)            │
│                │ Phase 2: Vertical scaling + read replicas      │
│                │ Phase 3: Extract hot modules to microservices  │
│                │   (Inventory + Finance first candidates)       │
├────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Testing        │ • Unit: xUnit + NSubstitute (Domain/App layer) │
│                │ • Integration: TestContainers (real Postgres)  │
│                │ • Architecture: NetArchTest (dependency rules)  │
│                │ • API: WebApplicationFactory + http tests       │
│                │ • Coverage target: 80%+ on core modules        │
│                │ • Mutation testing: Stryker.NET (critical path) │
└────────────────┴────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

text

PHASE 1 — MODULES & AUTOMATION
✅ IAM module (users, roles, permissions, JWT auth)
✅ Finance module (GL, AR, AP, bank recon, GST)
✅ Sales module (quotation → order → delivery → invoice)
✅ Purchase module (PR → RFQ → PO → GRN → 3-way match)
✅ Inventory module (stock ledger, entries, reorder, counts)
✅ CRM module (leads, opportunities, complaints, loyalty)

✅ Assets module (register, depreciation, maintenance)
✅ Reporting module (dashboards, standard reports, export)
✅ 5 automations explained (ELI10 + technical)
✅ Cross-cutting: audit log, notifications, attachments, outbox

PHASE 2 — DATABASE SCHEMA
✅ 35+ tables designed with full column definitions
✅ PKs (UUID), FKs, unique constraints, check constraints
✅ Composite indexes + partial indexes on hot paths
✅ Soft delete strategy (deleted_at column + query filters)
✅ Audit fields on all mutable tables
✅ Identity tables + custom user_profiles
✅ RBAC: roles, permissions, role_permissions
✅ Branch isolation strategy
✅ Outbox, audit_logs, notifications, attachments tables

PHASE 3 — API DESIGN
✅ JWT + HttpOnly refresh token strategy (justified)
✅ 50+ endpoints across all modules
✅ Request/Response DTOs defined
✅ Validation rules per endpoint
✅ Standard error format (RFC 7807)
✅ Standard success format with pagination meta
✅ API versioning (URL path: /api/v1/)
✅ OpenAPI/Swagger structure outline
✅ Idempotency-Key for payment endpoints

PHASE 4 — ARCHITECTURE
✅ Full solution folder structure
✅ Modular monolith with 8 bounded modules
✅ Clean Architecture 4-layer per module
✅ Strict dependency rules documented
✅ Cross-module communication via outbox + contracts
✅ EF Core for writes (aggregates, transactions)
✅ Dapper for reads (queries, reports, dashboards)
✅ Pipeline behaviors (validation, logging, transaction, audit)
✅ Hangfire for background jobs
✅ Code examples: Aggregate, Handler, Dapper query, Controller
✅ Program.cs wiring example

NON-FUNCTIONAL
✅ Performance targets (p95 <200ms reads, <500ms writes)
✅ Security (OWASP, TLS1.3, AES-256, JWT RS256)
✅ Compliance (GST e-invoice, TDS, PF/ESI, 7yr audit)
✅ Scalability path (monolith → microservices roadmap)
✅ Testing strategy (unit, integration, architecture, coverage 80%)
```

# CMR ERP — CRM Module: Complete Deep-Dive Blueprint

---

## Explicit Assumptions for CRM Module

text

```
✅ CRM = Customer Relationship Management (standalone module within CMR ERP)
✅ Industry = Retail / Distribution (multi-branch India)
✅ CRM Scope = Lead → Opportunity → Customer → Retention → Support
✅ Automation = Full pipeline automation + AI-like scoring
✅ Integration = Sales, Finance, HR, Inventory, Notification modules
✅ Channels = Email, SMS, WhatsApp, Phone, Walk-in
✅ Compliance = GDPR-lite (India PDPB), audit trail, consent tracking
✅ Users = Sales Reps, CRM Managers, Support Agents, Branch Managers
✅ Scale = 300 concurrent users, 50,000+ customer records
```

---

# PHASE 1 — CRM Module Map + All Sub-Modules

---

text

```

├──────────────┬──────────────┬──────────────┬────────────────────────┤

├──────────────┼──────────────┼──────────────┼────────────────────────┤

│ Management   │ Management   │ Rewards      │ Ticket Management      │
│              │              │              │                        │
│ Contact &    │ Quotation    │ Customer     │ SLA Management         │
│ Account Mgmt │ Tracking     │ Segmentation │                        │
│              │              │              │ Customer Feedback       │

## 1.2 Sub-Module 1: Lead Management

text

```
PURPOSE:
Captures potential customers from every channel and tracks
them until they become real buying opportunities.

ELI10:
Imagine you have a lemonade stand. A kid walks by and looks
at your stand but doesn't buy yet. That kid is a "lead"!
You write their name in your notebook and remember to follow
up with them later. Lead management is that notebook — but
for thousands of kids at once!

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

KEY FEATURES:
├── Multi-channel lead capture
│     ├── Web form / landing page
│     ├── WhatsApp / SMS keyword
│     ├── Walk-in registration
│     ├── Trade show / event import (CSV)
│     ├── Social media (Facebook Lead Ads API)
│     └── Manual entry by sales rep
├── Lead deduplication (phone + email matching)
├── Lead scoring (activity-based + profile-based)
├── Lead routing / assignment rules
├── Lead status pipeline
├── Lead source tracking (UTM parameters)
├── Follow-up scheduling + reminders
└── Lead conversion to Opportunity + Contact + Account

MAIN ENTITIES:
├── Lead
├── LeadSource
├── LeadScore
├── LeadActivity
├── LeadAssignmentRule
└── LeadTag

LEAD STATUS PIPELINE:
New → Contacted → Qualified → Converted
         ↓
      Unqualified (dead end)

MAIN WORKFLOWS:
├── Capture lead → Auto-score → Auto-assign to sales rep
├── Sales rep contacts lead → Log activity → Schedule next action
├── Qualify lead → Convert to Opportunity + Contact
├── Mark as unqualified → Archive with reason
└── Bulk import leads → Deduplicate → Assign batch
```

---

## 1.3 Sub-Module 2: Contact & Account Management

text

```
PURPOSE:
Stores complete 360° profiles of every person (Contact)
and every company (Account) we do business with.

ELI10:
It's like your school's address book — but super powerful.
It remembers not just their phone number but EVERYTHING:
what they bought, what they complained about, their birthday,
how much money they owe us, and even their favorite products!

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

KEY FEATURES:
├── Contact profiles (individual customers)
├── Account profiles (business/corporate customers)
├── 360° view: orders, payments, complaints, activities
├── Relationship mapping (contact belongs to account)
├── Custom fields per contact/account type
├── Tags + segments
├── Communication preferences (opt-in/opt-out)
├── Document attachments (KYC, agreements)
├── Duplicate detection + merge
└── Import / export (Excel / CSV)

MAIN ENTITIES:
├── Contact
├── Account (company)
├── ContactAccount (relationship)
├── ContactAddress
├── ContactCommunicationPreference
├── CustomField + CustomFieldValue
└── ContactMergeHistory

MAIN WORKFLOWS:
├── Create contact → Link to account → Set preferences
├── View 360° timeline: orders + payments + tickets + activities
├── Merge duplicates → Keep master record → Archive duplicate
├── Opt-out from marketing → Flag all future communications
└── Export contact list for campaign
```

---

## 1.4 Sub-Module 3: Opportunity & Pipeline Management

text

```
PURPOSE:
Tracks every potential sale from first interest to closed
deal. Helps managers see what's in the pipeline and forecast revenue.

ELI10:
Think of it like a board game. Each sales deal is a game piece
moving from START to FINISH. Some pieces move forward, some
get stuck, and some fall off. This sub-module is the board —
it shows where every deal is right now and how likely it is
to reach FINISH (= win the sale).

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

KEY FEATURES:
├── Opportunity stages (configurable Kanban pipeline)
├── Win probability % per stage
├── Expected revenue + weighted pipeline value
├── Competitor tracking
├── Products/services linked to opportunity
├── Proposal / quotation attachment
├── Activity log per opportunity
├── Stale deal alerts (no activity for N days)
├── Win / loss analysis with reasons
└── Forecast reports (by rep, branch, period)

OPPORTUNITY STAGES (configurable):
Prospecting (10%) → Qualification (25%) → Needs Analysis (40%)
→ Proposal (60%) → Negotiation (80%) → Closed Won (100%)
                                      → Closed Lost (0%)

MAIN ENTITIES:
├── Opportunity
├── OpportunityStage
├── OpportunityProduct
├── OpportunityCompetitor
├── OpportunityActivity
└── OpportunityForecast

MAIN WORKFLOWS:
├── Convert lead → Create opportunity → Set stage + value
├── Progress through stages → Update probability → Forecast updates
├── Attach quotation → Customer reviews → Negotiate
├── Close Won → Auto-create Sales Order in Sales module
├── Close Lost → Log reason → Move to nurture campaign
└── Manager reviews pipeline → Identifies stale deals → Escalates
```

---

## 1.5 Sub-Module 4: Activity & Task Management

text

```
PURPOSE:
Every call made, email sent, meeting held, or note written
about a customer gets recorded here. Nothing falls through cracks.

ELI10:
It's like a diary for the sales team. Every time you talk
to a customer, you write it down — "Called them on Monday,
they said they'll think about it, call again on Friday."
The computer reminds you on Friday automatically!

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

KEY FEATURES:
├── Activity types: Call, Email, Meeting, Demo, Visit, Note, Task
├── Log activities against Lead / Contact / Opportunity / Account
├── Scheduled activities with reminders
├── Overdue activity alerts
├── Activity timeline view (360° history)
├── Meeting calendar integration
├── Call outcome tracking (answered, voicemail, no-answer)
├── Email open/click tracking
└── Activity reports by rep / by team

MAIN ENTITIES:
├── Activity
├── ActivityType
├── ActivityOutcome
├── Task
└── CalendarEvent

MAIN WORKFLOWS:
├── Schedule call → Receive reminder → Make call → Log outcome
├── Send email → Track open/click → Auto-log → Follow-up if no reply
├── Log meeting → Attach notes → Create follow-up tasks
├── Overdue task → Escalation notification → Manager alert
└── Activity summary report → Weekly digest to managers
```

---

## 1.6 Sub-Module 5: Campaign Management

text

```
PURPOSE:
Plans and executes marketing campaigns (email, SMS, WhatsApp)
to targeted customer segments and tracks results.

ELI10:
Imagine you're having a birthday sale at your shop. You want
to tell all your customers about it. Instead of calling 1000
people one by one, the computer sends all of them a message
at once! And then it tells you how many people read it,
how many came to your shop, and how much money you made
from the sale.

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

KEY FEATURES:
├── Campaign types: Email, SMS, WhatsApp, Push notification
├── Audience targeting by segment / tag / custom filter
├── Campaign templates (WYSIWYG editor)
├── Scheduled + triggered campaigns
├── A/B testing (subject lines, content)
├── Campaign metrics: sent, delivered, opened, clicked, converted
├── Unsubscribe / opt-out handling (compliance)
├── Campaign ROI tracking (revenue generated)
└── Drip campaign (multi-step automated sequences)

MAIN ENTITIES:
├── Campaign
├── CampaignSegment
├── CampaignTemplate
├── CampaignRecipient
├── CampaignMetric
├── DripCampaign
└── DripCampaignStep

MAIN WORKFLOWS:
├── Define segment → Build template → Schedule campaign
├── Campaign sends → Track delivery → Track opens/clicks
├── Recipient clicks link → CTA action → Lead or Order created
├── Auto-unsubscribe on opt-out → Flag contact
└── Campaign ends → Generate ROI report → Compare with budget
```

---

## 1.7 Sub-Module 6: Loyalty & Rewards

text

```
PURPOSE:
Rewards customers for repeat purchases and referrals,
building long-term loyalty and increasing lifetime value.

ELI10:
You know how some shops give you a stamp card? After 10
stamps you get a free coffee! This works the same way —
every time you buy something, you get "points." When you
have enough points, you can use them to get a discount
or a free gift. It makes you want to come back!

━━━━��━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

KEY FEATURES:
├── Loyalty program tiers (Silver / Gold / Platinum)
├── Points earn rules (per rupee spent, per product, per event)
├── Points redemption rules (min threshold, partial redemption)
├── Referral program (refer a friend → earn bonus points)
├── Birthday / anniversary bonus points
├── Points expiry management
├── Tier upgrade / downgrade rules
├── Loyalty card / QR code generation
├── Points statement (like a mini bank statement)
└── Loyalty ROI dashboard

MAIN ENTITIES:
├── LoyaltyProgram
├── LoyaltyTier
├── LoyaltyAccount (per customer)
├── LoyaltyTransaction (earn / redeem / expire / adjust)
├── LoyaltyRule (earn rules)
├── RedemptionRule
├── ReferralProgram
└── ReferralTransaction

TIER STRUCTURE:
Bronze:  0 – 4,999 points     → 1 point per ₹100 spent
Silver:  5,000 – 14,999 pts   → 1.5 points per ₹100 spent
Gold:    15,000 – 49,999 pts  → 2 points per ₹100 spent
Platinum: 50,000+ pts         → 3 points per ₹100 spent + perks

MAIN WORKFLOWS:
├── Customer buys → Calculate points → Credit to account
├── Customer redeems → Validate balance → Deduct → Apply discount
├── Points expire → Notify customer → Auto-expire
├── Customer crosses tier threshold → Upgrade tier → Notify + reward
└── Referral made → New customer registers → Bonus credited to both
```

---

## 1.8 Sub-Module 7: Customer Segmentation

text

```
PURPOSE:
Groups customers into meaningful segments so marketing
and sales can target the right people with the right message.

ELI10:
Imagine you have 3 groups of friends:
Group 1 loves football, Group 2 loves cricket, Group 3 loves chess.
When a football match is on, you only invite Group 1.
Segmentation does the same for customers — it puts them
in groups so we send the RIGHT message to the RIGHT people!

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

KEY FEATURES:
├── RFM Analysis (Recency, Frequency, Monetary)
├── Static segments (manual list)
├── Dynamic segments (rule-based, auto-refreshed)
├── Behavioral segments (bought product X, visited N times)
├── Geographic segments (city, state, pincode)
├── Demographic segments (age, gender)
├── Segment overlap analysis
└── Export segment for campaigns

RFM SEGMENT EXAMPLES:
├── Champions: Bought recently, often, big spenders
├── Loyal: Buy often, decent spend
├── At Risk: Used to buy often, haven't recently
├── Lost: Haven't bought in 6+ months
└── New Customers: First purchase in last 30 days

MAIN ENTITIES:
├── Segment
├── SegmentRule
├── SegmentMember
└── RfmScore

MAIN WORKFLOWS:
├── Define segment rules → Run → Preview members → Save
├── Dynamic segment auto-refreshes daily → Members in/out
├── RFM job runs weekly → Scores every customer → Assigns segment
└── Segment fed into campaign → Track conversions back to segment
```

---

## 1.9 Sub-Module 8: Complaint & Ticket Management

text

```
PURPOSE:
Handles every customer complaint or service request from
opening to resolution, with SLAs enforced.

ELI10:
When you buy a toy and it breaks, you tell the shop.
The shop writes down your problem, promises to fix it in
2 days, and gives it to the repair person. This module is
like that notebook — it tracks every complaint and makes
sure no one forgets to fix it on time!

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

KEY FEATURES:
├── Ticket creation (any channel: email, phone, WhatsApp, walk-in)
├── Ticket categories + priorities (Low/Medium/High/Critical)
├── SLA rules per category + priority
├── Auto-assignment to support agents
├── Ticket escalation (SLA breach → supervisor)
├── Internal notes + customer-visible comments
├── Status tracking: Open → InProgress → Resolved → Closed
├── Customer satisfaction rating (CSAT after resolution)
├── Knowledge base articles linked to tickets
├── Ticket reports + SLA compliance dashboard
└── Repeat complaint detection

MAIN ENTITIES:
├── Ticket
├── TicketCategory
├── TicketComment
├── TicketEscalation
├── SlaRule
├── SlaTimer
├── CsatResponse
└── KnowledgeBaseArticle

SLA MATRIX EXAMPLE:
├── Critical: First response 1h, Resolution 4h
├── High:     First response 4h, Resolution 24h
├── Medium:   First response 8h, Resolution 48h
└── Low:      First response 24h, Resolution 72h

MAIN WORKFLOWS:
├── Customer raises complaint → Auto-create ticket → Assign agent
├── Agent picks up → Status: InProgress → Work on resolution
├── SLA timer runs → Breach warning at 75% → Alert agent/supervisor
├── SLA breached → Auto-escalate → Notify manager
├── Resolve ticket → Notify customer → Request CSAT rating
└── Closed ticket → Feed to knowledge base if reusable solution
```

---

## 1.10 Sub-Module 9: Feedback & Surveys

text

```
PURPOSE:
Collects structured feedback from customers about products,
services, and experiences to drive improvement.

MAIN ENTITIES:
├── Survey
├── SurveyQuestion
├── SurveyResponse
├── SurveyAnswer
└── NpsScore (Net Promoter Score)

NPS Categories:
Promoters (9-10) → Passives (7-8) → Detractors (0-6)

MAIN WORKFLOWS:
├── Define survey → Select audience → Schedule send
├── Customer receives link → Fills form → Submits
├── Responses aggregated → NPS calculated → Dashboard updated
└── Low NPS alert → CRM manager notified → Follow-up action
```

# PHASE 1B — CRM Automation Design

---

## AUTOMATION 1: Lead Scoring Engine

text

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ ELI10:                                                      │
│                                                             │
│ Imagine you're a teacher and 30 students raise their hands. │
│ You want to call on the ones who REALLY know the answer.    │
│ You look for signs: they're sitting up straight, they look  │
│ confident, they've answered correctly before.               │
│                                                             │
│ Lead scoring does the same! It gives each potential customer│
│ a score based on clues: Did they open our email? Did they   │
│ visit our website? Are they from a big company? The ones    │
│ with the highest score get called first by our sales team!  │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

TECHNICAL DESIGN:

SCORING RULES ENGINE:

Profile Score (max 50 points):
┌────────────────────────────┬────────┐
│ Signal                     │ Points │
├────────────────────────────┼────────┤
│ Has company/GST            │ +15    │
│ Known city (tier 1)        │ +10    │
│ Budget > ₹1 lakh           │ +10    │
│ Decision maker             │ +10    │
│ Right industry             │ +5     │
└────────────────────────────┴────────┘

Behavior Score (max 50 points):
┌────────────────────────────┬────────┐
│ Signal                     │ Points │
├────────────────────────────┼────────┤
│ Opens email campaign       │ +5     │
│ Clicks email link          │ +10    │
│ Requests demo              │ +20    │
│ Views pricing page         │ +15    │
│ Replies to sales rep       │ +10    │
│ No activity 30 days        │ -10    │
└────────────────────────────┴────────┘

TRIGGER:
  - Real-time: LeadActivityLoggedEvent → ScoreUpdateHandler
  - Batch: Hangfire daily job → Recalculate all scores
    (decay inactive leads)

HANDLER LOGIC:
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ LeadScoringService.RecalculateAsync(leadId):            │
│                                                         │
│ 1. Fetch lead profile fields → compute profile score    │
│ 2. Fetch activity log (last 90 days)                    │
│ 3. Apply behavior scoring rules from config table       │
│ 4. Apply decay: activities older than 30d = 50% weight  │
│ 5. Total = min(profileScore + behaviorScore, 100)       │
│ 6. Update lead_scores table                             │
│ 7. If score crosses threshold → raise ScoredHighEvent   │
│    → trigger routing review                             │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

ROUTING ON HIGH SCORE:
  LeadScore >= 75 → Auto-assign to senior sales rep
                 → Send rep: "Hot lead alert!" notification
                 → Schedule auto-call task within 2 hours
```

---

## AUTOMATION 2: Opportunity Stale Deal Alert

text

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ ELI10:                                                      │
│                                                             │
│ Imagine you're building a LEGO set and you stop for a week. │
│ Your mom checks and says "Hey! You haven't touched this in  │
│ a week — do you still want to finish it?"                   │
│                                                             │
│ The ERP does the same with sales deals. If a salesperson    │
│ hasn't done anything with a deal in 7 days, the system      │
│ automatically taps them on the shoulder and says "Hey!      │
│ Don't forget about this customer!"                          │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

TECHNICAL DESIGN:

Hangfire RecurringJob: Cron = "0 8 * * *" (8AM daily)

StaleDealDetectionJob.ExecuteAsync():
  1. Query opportunities WHERE:
       status = 'Open'
       AND last_activity_date < NOW() - INTERVAL '{N} days'
       AND stale_alert_sent_at IS NULL
       OR stale_alert_sent_at < NOW() - INTERVAL '3 days'

  2. N = configurable per stage:
       Prospecting: 3 days stale
       Qualification: 5 days stale
       Proposal: 7 days stale
       Negotiation: 10 days stale

  3. For each stale opportunity:
       → Create overdue Task for assigned sales rep
       → Send InApp + Email notification to rep
       → If stale > 2x threshold → notify sales manager too
       → Update stale_alert_sent_at = NOW()
       → Log to audit_logs

  4. If deal stale for 30+ days → mark as "AtRisk"
       → Add to manager's review dashboard widget
```

---

## AUTOMATION 3: Loyalty Points Auto-Award

text

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ ELI10:                                                      │
│                                                             │
│ Remember the stamp card at the pizza shop? Every time you   │
│ buy a pizza, the waiter stamps your card automatically —    │
│ you don't have to ask them! This automation does the same:  │
│ whenever a customer pays their bill in our shop, the computer│
│ immediately adds loyalty points to their account. They see  │
│ "Congrats! You earned 150 points!" on their phone.          │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

TECHNICAL DESIGN:

TRIGGER:
  PaymentReceivedIntegrationEvent (from Finance module via Outbox)
  → LoyaltyPointsAwardHandler

HANDLER:
  LoyaltyPointsAwardHandler.Handle(event):
    1. Find LoyaltyAccount for customer_id
    2. If no account → Create new Bronze account
    3. Fetch active LoyaltyRule for customer's tier + purchase
    4. Calculate points:
         points = FLOOR(payment.amount / rule.spend_per_point)
                 * rule.points_multiplier
    5. Check for bonus rules:
         - Is birthday month? → bonus_multiplier = 2x
         - Is campaign active? → apply campaign multiplier
    6. Insert LoyaltyTransaction (type='Earn')
    7. Update LoyaltyAccount.balance += points
    8. Check tier upgrade:
         IF new_balance >= next_tier.min_points THEN
           Upgrade tier → notify customer
    9. Send notification:
         "🎉 You earned {points} points! Balance: {total}"
    10. Post outbox message for SMS/WhatsApp delivery

POINTS EXPIRY JOB:
  Hangfire: Cron = "0 1 1 * *" (1AM, 1st of every month)
  
  PointsExpiryJob:
    SELECT loyalty_transactions
    WHERE type = 'Earn'
    AND earned_at < NOW() - INTERVAL '12 months'  (configurable)
    AND is_expired = FALSE
    
    For each expiring batch:
      → Calculate unexpired points from that batch
      → Insert LoyaltyTransaction (type='Expire', amount=-N)
      → Update balance
      → Notify customer 30 days before expiry (warning job)
      → Notify customer 7 days before expiry (final warning)
```

---

## AUTOMATION 4: Campaign Trigger & Drip Automation

text

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ ELI10:                                                      │
│                                                             │
│ Imagine when you join a new school, the first week your     │
│ teacher sends you the school rules. Next week, she tells you│
│ about clubs. The week after, about sports. It's a plan!     │
│                                                             │
│ Drip campaigns work the same way. When a new customer signs │
│ up, they automatically get:                                 │
│ Day 1: "Welcome! Here's 10% off your first order"           │
│ Day 3: "Did you know we have these great products?"         │
│ Day 7: "Other customers love this item!"                    │
│ Day 14: "We miss you! Here's a special offer"               │
│ All automatic, no one needs to remember!                    │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

TECHNICAL DESIGN:

DRIP CAMPAIGN STATE MACHINE:

[Trigger] → [Step 1 Email] 
              ↓ (delay N days)
          → [Step 2 SMS]
              ↓ (if email opened) OR (if not opened → different step)
          → [Step 3 WhatsApp]
              ↓ (if purchased → exit drip)
              ↓ (if not → Step 4 Discount offer)
              ↓
          → [Complete / Exit]

TRIGGER TYPES (configurable):
  - CustomerCreated → Welcome drip
  - LeadConverted → Onboarding drip
  - No purchase in 30 days → Re-engagement drip
  - After purchase → Cross-sell drip
  - Post complaint resolved → Win-back drip
  - Birthday (7 days before) → Birthday offer drip

HANGFIRE JOB: DripCampaignProcessor (every 5 minutes)
  
  1. SELECT drip_campaign_enrollments WHERE:
         next_step_at <= NOW()
         AND status = 'Active'
  
  2. For each enrollment:
       a. Get next DripCampaignStep
       b. Check step conditions:
            - If condition = "email_not_opened" AND email was opened
              → skip this step, move to alternate branch
       c. Execute step action:
            - channel = 'Email' → queue SendGrid job
            - channel = 'SMS' → queue MSG91 job
            - channel = 'WhatsApp' → queue WhatsApp API job
       d. Update enrollment:
            - current_step_id = next_step_id
            - next_step_at = NOW() + step.delay_hours
       e. Log CampaignRecipient metric
  
  3. Check exit conditions:
       - Customer made purchase? → Exit drip, mark converted
       - Customer unsubscribed? → Exit drip, mark opted-out
       - All steps completed? → Mark completed

A/B TESTING ENGINE:
  When campaign has variants:
    - Randomly assign recipient to variant A or B (50/50 or configured)
    - Track open rate, click rate, conversion per variant
    - After minimum sample size reached (configurable, e.g. 100 recipients)
    - Auto-select winning variant → send remaining recipients winner
```

---

## AUTOMATION 5: Ticket SLA Enforcement

text

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ ELI10:                                                      │
│                                                             │
│ Imagine you ordered a birthday cake and the shop promised   │
│ to deliver it by 5pm. At 4pm, the shop's alarm rings:       │
│ "Only 1 hour left! Is the cake ready?" If it's not ready    │
│ by 5pm, their boss gets a phone call automatically!         │
│                                                             │
│ SLA automation does the same for customer complaints.        │
│ The system knows "this must be fixed in 4 hours." It warns  │
│ the agent at 3 hours and calls the manager if not done      │
│ by hour 4.                                                  │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

TECHNICAL DESIGN:

SLA STATES PER TICKET:
  [Running] → [Warning at 75%] → [Breached] → [Escalated]
  
  At any point: [Paused] (customer awaiting info)
                → [Resumed] (customer replied)

HANGFIRE JOB: SlaMonitorJob (every 2 minutes)

  1. SELECT tickets WHERE
         status NOT IN ('Resolved','Closed')
         AND sla_due_at IS NOT NULL
         AND sla_breach_notified = FALSE

  2. For each ticket:
       a. Calculate time_remaining = sla_due_at - NOW()
       
       b. If time_remaining <= (sla_duration * 0.25) AND warning not sent:
            → Send warning notification to assigned agent
            → Set sla_warning_sent_at = NOW()
       
       c. If NOW() > sla_due_at AND not escalated:
            → Mark ticket sla_status = 'Breached'
            → Auto-escalate: find next tier agent/manager
            → Create EscalationRecord
            → Send urgent notification to escalated_to
            → Send notification to customer:
                 "We apologize for the delay. A senior agent will 
                  contact you within the next {N} hours."
            → Set sla_breach_notified = TRUE
       
       d. Log all SLA events to sla_timers table

  PAUSE/RESUME:
    Ticket updated with status = 'WaitingOnCustomer':
      → Pause SLA timer: record paused_at
      → sla_due_at stays the same (paused time not counted)
    
    Customer replies:
      → Resume SLA: paused_duration calculated
      → sla_due_at += paused_duration (extend by pause time)
      → Resume timer

ESCALATION MATRIX TABLE:
┌──────────────┬──────────────────┬────────────────────────┐
│ Priority     │ Level 1 (breach) │ Level 2 (+2hr breach)  │
├──────────────┼──────────────────┼────────────────────────┤
│ Critical     │ Support Lead     │ Branch Manager + SMS    │
│ High         │ Support Lead     │ CRM Manager             │
│ Medium       │ CRM Manager      │ Branch Manager          │
│ Low          │ CRM Manager      │ (no auto-escalation)    │
└──────────────┴──────────────────┴────────────────────────┘
```

---

## AUTOMATION 6: RFM Segmentation Auto-Refresh

text

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ ELI10:                                                      │
│                                                             │
│ Imagine grading 1000 students every week. You give grades:  │
│ A (Champions), B (Good), C (Average), D (At Risk), F (Lost).│
│ The grades change every week based on what students did.    │
│ This automation does that for customers automatically!      │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

TECHNICAL DESIGN:

Hangfire RecurringJob: "0 3 * * 0" (3AM every Sunday)

RfmSegmentationJob.ExecuteAsync():

  -- PostgreSQL Query for RFM calculation
  1. Calculate R, F, M scores for all customers:
  
     Recency (R): Days since last purchase
       ≤ 30 days → R=5
       31-60    → R=4
       61-90    → R=3
       91-180   → R=2
       > 180    → R=1
     
     Frequency (F): Number of orders in 12 months
       ≥ 12     → F=5
       8-11     → F=4
       4-7      → F=3
       2-3      → F=2
       1        → F=1
     
     Monetary (M): Total spend in 12 months
       ≥ ₹50,000  → M=5
       ₹20-49,999  → M=4
       ₹10-19,999  → M=3
       ₹5-9,999   → M=2
       < ₹5,000   → M=1
  
  2. Assign segment based on R+F+M combination:
       R≥4, F≥4, M≥4 → "Champions"
       R≥3, F≥3       → "Loyal"
       R≥4, F≤2       → "New Customers"
       R≤2, F≥3       → "At Risk"
       R=1, F≤2, M≤2  → "Lost"
       Others         → "Potential Loyalists"
  
  3. Update rfm_scores table
  4. Update segment_members (remove old, add new)
  5. Detect segment changes:
       Old: Loyal → New: At Risk → Alert CRM manager
       Old: At Risk → New: Champions → Celebrate! Log improvement
  6. Trigger "At Risk" re-engagement campaign automatically
```
