### Example 3: Dapper Query Handler

csharp

```
// CMR.Modules.Sales.Application/Orders/Queries/GetSalesOrders/
// GetSalesOrdersHandler.cs

public sealed record GetSalesOrdersQuery : IQuery<PagedResult<SalesOrderListItem>>
{
    public int Page { get; init; } = 1;
    public int PageSize { get; init; } = 20;
    public string? Search { get; init; }
    public string? Status { get; init; }
    public Guid? CustomerId { get; init; }
    public DateOnly? FromDate { get; init; }
    public DateOnly? ToDate { get; init; }
    public string SortBy { get; init; } = "order_date";
    public string SortDir { get; init; } = "desc";
}

public sealed class GetSalesOrdersHandler
    : IQueryHandler<GetSalesOrdersQuery, PagedResult<SalesOrderListItem>>
{
    private readonly IDbConnectionFactory _connectionFactory;
    private readonly ICurrentUserService _currentUser;

    public GetSalesOrdersHandler(
        IDbConnectionFactory connectionFactory,
        ICurrentUserService currentUser)
    {
        _connectionFactory = connectionFactory;
        _currentUser = currentUser;
    }

    public async Task<PagedResult<SalesOrderListItem>> Handle(
        GetSalesOrdersQuery query,
        CancellationToken cancellationToken)
    {
        // Dapper: raw SQL for maximum performance on reads
        const string sql = """
            WITH filtered AS (
                SELECT
                    so.id,
                    so.order_number,
                    so.order_date,
                    c.name         AS customer_name,
                    c.code         AS customer_code,
                    c.phone        AS customer_phone,
                    so.total_amount,
                    so.status,
                    so.delivery_date,
                    up.full_name   AS created_by_name,
                    so.created_at,
                    COUNT(soi.id)  AS item_count,
                    COUNT(*)       OVER() AS total_count
                FROM sales_orders so
                INNER JOIN customers c ON c.id = so.customer_id
                INNER JOIN user_profiles up ON up.user_id = so.created_by
                LEFT JOIN sales_order_items soi ON soi.sales_order_id = so.id
                WHERE
                    so.branch_id = @BranchId
                    AND so.deleted_at IS NULL
                    AND (@Status IS NULL OR so.status = @Status)
                    AND (@CustomerId IS NULL OR so.customer_id = @CustomerId)
                    AND (@FromDate IS NULL OR so.order_date >= @FromDate)
                    AND (@ToDate IS NULL OR so.order_date <= @ToDate)
                    AND (
                        @Search IS NULL
                        OR so.order_number ILIKE @SearchPattern
                        OR c.name ILIKE @SearchPattern
                        OR c.code ILIKE @SearchPattern
                    )
                GROUP BY
                    so.id, so.order_number, so.order_date,
                    c.name, c.code, c.phone,
                    so.total_amount, so.status, so.delivery_date,
                    up.full_name, so.created_at
            )
            SELECT *
            FROM filtered
            ORDER BY
                CASE WHEN @SortBy = 'order_date' AND @SortDir = 'asc'
                    THEN order_date END ASC,
                CASE WHEN @SortBy = 'order_date' AND @SortDir = 'desc'
                    THEN order_date END DESC,
                CASE WHEN @SortBy = 'order_number' AND @SortDir = 'asc'
                    THEN order_number END ASC,
                CASE WHEN @SortBy = 'total_amount' AND @SortDir = 'desc'
                    THEN total_amount END DESC
            LIMIT @PageSize OFFSET @Offset;
        """;

        using var connection = _connectionFactory.CreateConnection();

        var parameters = new
        {
            BranchId = _currentUser.BranchId,
            query.Status,
            query.CustomerId,
            FromDate = query.FromDate?.ToDateTime(TimeOnly.MinValue),
            ToDate = query.ToDate?.ToDateTime(TimeOnly.MaxValue),
            Search = query.Search,
            SearchPattern = string.IsNullOrEmpty(query.Search)
                ? null
                : $"%{query.Search}%",
            SortBy = query.SortBy,
            SortDir = query.SortDir,
            PageSize = query.PageSize,
            Offset = (query.Page - 1) * query.PageSize
        };

        var rows = await connection.QueryAsync<SalesOrderListItem, long, (SalesOrderListItem, long)>(
            sql,
            (item, totalCount) => (item, totalCount),
            parameters,
            splitOn: "total_count");

        var resultList = rows.ToList();
        var totalCount = resultList.FirstOrDefault().Item2;

        return PagedResult<SalesOrderListItem>.Create(
            resultList.Select(r => r.Item1).ToList(),
            totalCount,
            query.Page,
            query.PageSize);
    }
}
```

### Example 4: Controller Endpoint

csharp

```
// CMR.Modules.Sales.Api/Controllers/SalesOrdersController.cs

[ApiController]
[Route("api/v1/sales/orders")]
[Authorize]
[Tags("Sales")]
public sealed class SalesOrdersController : ControllerBase
{
    private readonly ISender _sender;      // MediatR

    public SalesOrdersController(ISender sender)
    {
        _sender = sender;
    }

    /// <summary>List sales orders with pagination and filters</summary>
    [HttpGet]
    [Authorize(Policy = "Sales.Order.View")]
    [ProducesResponseType(typeof(PagedResult<SalesOrderListItem>), 200)]
    public async Task<IActionResult> GetAll(
        [FromQuery] GetSalesOrdersQuery query,
        CancellationToken cancellationToken)
    {
        var result = await _sender.Send(query, cancellationToken);
        return Ok(ApiResponse.Success(result));
    }

    /// <summary>Create a new sales order (draft)</summary>
    [HttpPost]
    [Authorize(Policy = "Sales.Order.Create")]
    [ProducesResponseType(typeof(SalesOrderResponse), 201)]
    [ProducesResponseType(typeof(ProblemDetails), 422)]
    public async Task<IActionResult> Create(
        [FromBody] CreateSalesOrderRequest request,
        CancellationToken cancellationToken)
    {
        var command = new CreateSalesOrderCommand
        {
            CustomerId = request.CustomerId,
            WarehouseId = request.WarehouseId,
            DeliveryDate = request.DeliveryDate,
            Notes = request.Notes,
            Items = request.Items.Select(i => new OrderItemDto
            {
                ProductId = i.ProductId,
                Quantity = i.Quantity,
                UnitPrice = i.UnitPrice,
                DiscountPct = i.DiscountPct
            }).ToList()
        };

        var result = await _sender.Send(command, cancellationToken);

        return CreatedAtAction(
            nameof(GetById),
            new { id = result.Id },
            ApiResponse.Success(result));
    }

    /// <summary>Get sales order by ID</summary>
    [HttpGet("{id:guid}")]
    [Authorize(Policy = "Sales.Order.View")]
    [ProducesResponseType(typeof(SalesOrderResponse), 200)]
    [ProducesResponseType(typeof(ProblemDetails), 404)]
    public async Task<IActionResult> GetById(
        Guid id,
        CancellationToken cancellationToken)
    {
        var result = await _sender.Send(
            new GetSalesOrderByIdQuery(id), cancellationToken);

        return result is null
            ? NotFound(ProblemDetailsFactory.CreateNotFound(id))
            : Ok(ApiResponse.Success(result));
    }

    /// <summary>Confirm a draft order</summary>
    [HttpPost("{id:guid}/confirm")]
    [Authorize(Policy = "Sales.Order.Confirm")]
    [ProducesResponseType(204)]
    [ProducesResponseType(typeof(ProblemDetails), 409)]
    public async Task<IActionResult> Confirm(
        Guid id,
        CancellationToken cancellationToken)
    {
        await _sender.Send(
            new ConfirmSalesOrderCommand(id), cancellationToken);

        return NoContent();
    }

    /// <summary>Cancel an order</summary>
    [HttpPost("{id:guid}/cancel")]
    [Authorize(Policy = "Sales.Order.Cancel")]
    [ProducesResponseType(204)]
    public async Task<IActionResult> Cancel(
        Guid id,
        [FromBody] CancelSalesOrderRequest request,
        CancellationToken cancellationToken)
    {
        await _sender.Send(
            new CancelSalesOrderCommand(id, request.Reason),
            cancellationToken);

        return NoContent();
    }
}

### Example 5: Module Communication (Domain Events via Outbox)

csharp

```
// When a SalesOrder is Confirmed → Inventory must reserve stock

// Step 1: Domain Event (in Sales.Domain)
public sealed record SalesOrderConfirmedEvent(
    Guid OrderId,
    Guid CustomerId,
    IReadOnlyList<OrderItemReservation> Items,
    Money TotalAmount) : IDomainEvent;

// Step 2: Event Handler in Sales.Application
// Converts domain event → outbox message (cross-module)
public class SalesOrderConfirmedEventHandler
    : IDomainEventHandler<SalesOrderConfirmedEvent>
{
    private readonly IOutboxService _outbox;

    public async Task Handle(
        SalesOrderConfirmedEvent notification,
        CancellationToken cancellationToken)
    {
        // Publish to outbox (not directly to Inventory module!)
        var integrationEvent = new StockReservationRequestedEvent(
            notification.OrderId,
            notification.Items.Select(i => new StockReservationItem(
                i.ProductId, i.WarehouseId, i.Quantity)).ToList());

        await _outbox.PublishAsync(integrationEvent, cancellationToken);
    }
}

// Step 3: Outbox Processor (Hangfire job, every 10 seconds)
public class OutboxProcessor : IRecurringJob
{
    public async Task ExecuteAsync()
    {
        // Read pending outbox messages
        // Dispatch to registered handlers by message type
        // Mark as sent / handle retries
    }
}

// Step 4: Inventory module subscribes to the event
// CMR.Modules.Inventory.Application/.../StockReservationHandler.cs
public class StockReservationRequestedHandler
    : IIntegrationEventHandler<StockReservationRequestedEvent>
{
    // Inventory module processes this independently
    // No direct code reference to Sales module!
}

### Example 6: Program.cs Wiring

csharp

```
// src/Api/Program.cs

var builder = WebApplication.CreateBuilder(args);

// -- Infrastructure --
builder.Services
    .AddPostgreSQL(builder.Configuration)       // DbContext + Dapper
    .AddIdentityAndJwt(builder.Configuration)   // ASP.NET Identity + JWT
    .AddHangfire(builder.Configuration)         // Background jobs
    .AddRedisCache(builder.Configuration)       // Caching
    .AddHttpContextAccessor()
    .AddMediatR(cfg => cfg.RegisterServicesFromAssemblies(
        typeof(CreateSalesOrderHandler).Assembly,
        typeof(CreateStockEntryHandler).Assembly
        // ... all module application assemblies
    ))
    .AddValidatorsFromAssemblies([...])
    .AddFluentValidation();

// -- Module registrations --
builder.Services
    .AddSalesModule(builder.Configuration)
    .AddInventoryModule(builder.Configuration)
    .AddFinanceModule(builder.Configuration)
    .AddPurchaseModule(builder.Configuration)
    .AddHRModule(builder.Configuration)
    .AddCRMModule(builder.Configuration)
    .AddIAMModule(builder.Configuration);

// -- Cross-cutting --
builder.Services
    .AddPipelineBehaviors()   // Validation + Logging + Transaction + Audit
    .AddCors(builder.Configuration)
    .AddRateLimiting()
    .AddApiVersioning()
    .AddSwagger();

var app = builder.Build();

// -- Middleware pipeline (ORDER MATTERS!) --
app.UseCorrelationId();
app.UseRequestLogging();
app.UseExceptionHandling();
app.UseHttpsRedirection();
app.UseCors();
app.UseRateLimiter();
app.UseAuthentication();
app.UseAuthorization();
app.UseBranchContext();        // Sets current branch from JWT claim
app.MapControllers();
app.UseHangfireDashboard("/jobs");
app.UseSwagger();

// -- Scheduled jobs --
RecurringJob.AddOrUpdate<StockReorderJob>(
    "stock-reorder-sweep",
    job => job.ExecuteAsync(),
    Cron.Daily(2));             // 2AM daily

RecurringJob.AddOrUpdate<PayrollRunJob>(
    "monthly-payroll",
    job => job.ExecuteAsync(),
    "0 2 28 * *");              // 28th every month

RecurringJob.AddOrUpdate<BankReconciliationJob>(
    "bank-recon",
    job => job.ExecuteAsync(),
    Cron.Daily(6));             // 6AM daily

RecurringJob.AddOrUpdate<OutboxProcessorJob>(
    "outbox-processor",
    job => job.ExecuteAsync(),
    "*/10 * * * * *");          // Every 10 seconds

app.Run();
```

# Non-Functional Requirements

## NFR Summary Table

text

```
┌────────────────┬────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ NFR Category   │ Specification                                  │
├────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Performance    │ • API p95 < 200ms (reads), < 500ms (writes)   │
│                │ • Report generation < 5s                       │
│                │ • DB queries < 50ms (indexed paths)            │
│                │ • Support 300 concurrent users                 │
├────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Availability   │ • 99.5% uptime SLA                            │
│                │ • Max 4 hours downtime/month                   │
│                │ • Auto health checks + restart (K8s/ECS)       │
├────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Security       │ • OWASP Top 10 compliance                      │
│                │ • TLS 1.3 in transit                           │
│                │ • AES-256 at rest (PAN, phone, bank no.)       │
│                │ • Rate limiting (100 req/min per user)         │
│                │ • Input validation on all endpoints            │
│                │ • SQL injection: parameterized queries only     │
│                │ • JWT: RS256 signing, 15-min access token      │
│                │ • Refresh tokens: rotated + revocable          │
├────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Compliance     │ • GST: e-invoice IRN generation via NIC API   │
│                │ • TDS: Form 16, 26Q automated                  │
│                │ • PF/ESI: challan generation                   │
│                │ • Audit trail: 7 years retention               │
│                │ • Data residency: India (Mumbai region)        │
├────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Scalability    │ Phase 1: Modular monolith (current)            │
│                │ Phase 2: Vertical scaling + read replicas      │
│                │ Phase 3: Extract hot modules to microservices  │
│                │   (Inventory + Finance first candidates)       │
├────────────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Testing        │ • Unit: xUnit + NSubstitute (Domain/App layer) │
│                │ • Integration: TestContainers (real Postgres)  │
│                │ • Architecture: NetArchTest (dependency rules)  │
│                │ • API: WebApplicationFactory + http tests       │
│                │ • Coverage target: 80%+ on core modules        │
│                │ • Mutation testing: Stryker.NET (critical path) │
└────────────────┴────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

# Final Deliverables

## ✅ Design Checklist

text

```
PHASE 1 — MODULES & AUTOMATION
✅ IAM module (users, roles, permissions, JWT auth)
✅ Finance module (GL, AR, AP, bank recon, GST)
✅ Sales module (quotation → order → delivery → invoice)
✅ Purchase module (PR → RFQ → PO → GRN → 3-way match)
✅ Inventory module (stock ledger, entries, reorder, counts)
✅ CRM module (leads, opportunities, complaints, loyalty)
✅ HR module (employees, attendance, leaves, payroll)
✅ Assets module (register, depreciation, maintenance)
✅ Reporting module (dashboards, standard reports, export)
✅ 5 automations explained (ELI10 + technical)
✅ Cross-cutting: audit log, notifications, attachments, outbox

PHASE 2 — DATABASE SCHEMA
✅ 35+ tables designed with full column definitions
✅ PKs (UUID), FKs, unique constraints, check constraints
✅ Composite indexes + partial indexes on hot paths
✅ Soft delete strategy (deleted_at column + query filters)
✅ Audit fields on all mutable tables
✅ Identity tables + custom user_profiles
✅ RBAC: roles, permissions, role_permissions
✅ Branch isolation strategy
✅ Outbox, audit_logs, notifications, attachments tables

PHASE 3 — API DESIGN
✅ JWT + HttpOnly refresh token strategy (justified)
✅ 50+ endpoints across all modules
✅ Request/Response DTOs defined
✅ Validation rules per endpoint
✅ Standard error format (RFC 7807)
✅ Standard success format with pagination meta
✅ API versioning (URL path: /api/v1/)
✅ OpenAPI/Swagger structure outline
✅ Idempotency-Key for payment endpoints

PHASE 4 — ARCHITECTURE
✅ Full solution folder structure
✅ Modular monolith with 8 bounded modules
✅ Clean Architecture 4-layer per module
✅ Strict dependency rules documented
✅ Cross-module communication via outbox + contracts
✅ EF Core for writes (aggregates, transactions)
✅ Dapper for reads (queries, reports, dashboards)
✅ Pipeline behaviors (validation, logging, transaction, audit)
✅ Hangfire for background jobs
✅ Code examples: Aggregate, Handler, Dapper query, Controller
✅ Program.cs wiring example

NON-FUNCTIONAL
✅ Performance targets (p95 <200ms reads, <500ms writes)
✅ Security (OWASP, TLS1.3, AES-256, JWT RS256)
✅ Compliance (GST e-invoice, TDS, PF/ESI, 7yr audit)
✅ Scalability path (monolith → microservices roadmap)
✅ Testing strategy (unit, integration, architecture, coverage 80%)
```

## 🗺️ Implementation Roadmap

text

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ MVP  (Month 1–3)                                               │

├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤

│ ✔ Products: Catalog, categories, pricing                       │
│ ✔ Inventory: Basic stock tracking, GRN                         │
│ ✔ Sales: Order → Invoice → Payment received                    │
│ ✔ Purchase: PO → GRN → Supplier payment                        │
│ ✔ Finance: Chart of accounts, manual journals, AR/AP           │
│ ✔ Basic reports: Sales summary, stock summary                  │
│ ✔ Infrastructure: CI/CD, PostgreSQL, Docker, monitoring        │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ V1  (Month 4–6)                                                │
│ "Full operations + compliance"                                  │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ ✔ GST: e-invoice (IRN), GSTR-1, GSTR-3B                       │
│ ✔ HR: Employee master, attendance, leave management            │
│ ✔ Payroll: Computation, payslips, TDS, PF/ESI                 │
│ ✔ Approval workflows: PR, PO, expense claims                   │
│ ✔ Bank reconciliation (upload + auto-matching)                 │
│ ✔ Notifications: Email + SMS + in-app                          │
│ ✔ Document attachments (S3)                                    │
│ ✔ Audit logs (full)                                            │
│ ✔ Multi-branch dashboards                                      │
│ ✔ Advanced inventory: batch/serial, inter-branch transfer      │
│ ✔ Auto stock reorder                                           │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ V2  (Month 7–12)                                               │
│ "Intelligence + growth features"                                │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ ✔ CRM: Leads, pipeline, opportunities, loyalty program         │
│ ✔ Asset management: Register, depreciation, maintenance        │
│ ✔ Advanced analytics: P&L, Balance Sheet, Cash Flow (live)     │
│ ✔ Custom report builder                                        │
│ ✔ Mobile app (React Native / Flutter) for field teams          │
│ ✔ WhatsApp Business API (invoices, payments)                   │
│ ✔ Payment gateway (Razorpay/PayU) for customer payments        │
│ ✔ Budgeting module                                             │
│ ✔ Performance appraisals                                       │
│ ✔ API rate limiting + throttling                               │
│ ✔ Read replica for reporting queries                           │
│ ✔ Scalability: Consider extracting Inventory/Finance           │
│   as separate services if load demands it                       │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

> - **50+ REST endpoints** with full DTO specs

You are a world-class ERP system architect and principal .NET solution designer.

Context  
Design a complete ERP system for “CMR” (define CMR as: [insert what CMR stands for: company type, industry, size, locations, countries, number of users, online/offline needs]).  
If any important detail is missing, ask up to 10 clarifying questions first, then proceed with reasonable assumptions and list them explicitly.

Goals  
I need an end-to-end ERP blueprint including:

All ERP modules (core + optional)  
Automation design explained so a 10-year-old child can understand  
Database schema (PostgreSQL) with tables, keys, constraints, indexes  
API design (RESTful .NET Web API) with endpoints + DTOs  
Modular Monolithic Clean Architecture in .NET using:  
ASP.NET Core Web API  
PostgreSQL  
ASP.NET Core Identity (RBAC)  
EF Core (migrations + write model)  
Dapper (read model / reporting)  
Clear boundaries between modules (no tight coupling)  
Output Format (deliver in the order below)  
Phase 1 — ERP Modules + Automation (ELI10 + professional view)  
Provide a module list grouped by domains (examples: Sales, Purchase, Inventory, Finance, HR, Projects, CRM, Asset, Manufacturing if applicable, Reporting).  
For each module include:  
Purpose (1–2 lines)  
Key features  
Main entities (nouns)  
Main workflows (verbs)  
Automation:  
List top automation scenarios (e.g., “Auto reorder stock”, “Invoice generation”, “Approval workflows”, “Payroll scheduling”)  
Explain each automation twice:  
Like I’m 10 years old  
Technical explanation (events, rules, triggers, background jobs)  
Include cross-cutting features:  
Multi-tenant (if applicable)  
Audit log  
Notifications (email/SMS/in-app)  
Attachments/documents  
Localization/currency/timezone (if applicable)  
Phase 2 — Domain Model + PostgreSQL Database Schema  
Provide:  
High-level ERD description (text-based is fine)  
Table-by-table schema with:  
Columns + data types  
PK/FK  
Unique constraints  
Indexes (including composite indexes where needed)  
Soft delete strategy (if used)  
Audit fields (CreatedAt, CreatedBy, etc.)  
Include:  
Identity tables approach (ASP.NET Core Identity + custom user profile)  
RBAC mapping (roles/permissions)  
Tenant isolation strategy (if multi-tenant)  
Explain key tradeoffs (normalization vs reporting tables, etc.)  
Phase 3 — API Design (ASP.NET Core Web API)  
For each module:

List REST endpoints with:  
Method + route  
Request DTO  
Response DTO  
Validation rules  
Errors (standard format)  
Include:  
Auth flow (Identity + JWT or cookie—choose and justify)  
Authorization strategy (roles/permissions)  
Pagination, filtering, sorting conventions  
Idempotency for critical endpoints (e.g., payments)  
API versioning strategy  
Example: OpenAPI/Swagger structure outline  
Phase 4 — Modular Monolith Clean Architecture (.NET)  
Design a modular monolith with clear module boundaries.

Provide solution structure, e.g.:  
/src/Api  
/src/BuildingBlocks (shared kernel)  
/src/Modules/Sales  
/src/Modules/Inventory  
etc.  
For each module define:  
Layers: Domain, Application, Infrastructure, API  
Dependency rules (what can reference what)  
How modules communicate (domain events, integration events, outbox pattern if needed)  
Data access pattern:  
EF Core for writes (Aggregate roots, transactions)  
Dapper for reads (query models, reporting views)  
Explain when to use EF vs Dapper  
Cross-cutting:  
Logging, tracing, exception handling middleware  
Background jobs (Hangfire/Quartz—pick one)  
Caching (optional)  
Outbox/inbox pattern (if needed)  
Include minimal example code snippets:  
One Aggregate + EF configuration  
One command handler  
One Dapper query  
One controller endpoint  
Non-Functional Requirements (explicitly address)  
Performance targets (assume reasonable defaults if not provided)  
Security (OWASP, PII, encryption at rest/in transit)  
Auditing and compliance  
Scalability path (modular monolith now, microservices later if needed)  
Testing strategy (unit/integration/contract tests)  
Final Deliverables  
End with:

A summarized checklist of what was designed  
A recommended implementation roadmap (MVP → v1 → v2)

Industry (retail/education/healthcare/construction/etc.)  
Number of branches  
Number of concurrent users  
Offline needs

claude-haiku-4-5-20251001

# Clarifying Questions Before I Design Your CMR ERP

Before I dive into a full blueprint, I need to understand your **CMR** context better. Please answer as many of these as possible:

## 1. **Business Identity**

- What does **CMR stand for**? (e.g., Customer Relationship Management, or is it an acronym for your company?)
- What **industry/vertical**? (e.g., retail, manufacturing, logistics, education, healthcare, construction, FMCG, e-commerce, services, etc.)
- Is this a **B2B**, **B2C**, or **B2B2C** business?

## 2. **Scale & Geography**

- How many **branches/locations** across how many **countries**?
- How many **concurrent active users** do you expect? (e.g., 50, 500, 5,000?)
- How many **daily transactions** (orders, invoices, etc.) on average?

## 3. **Core Business Processes**

- What are your **top 3 pain points** today? (e.g., manual invoicing, inventory mismatches, slow approvals?)
- Do you have **manufacturing/assembly processes**, or purely trade/services?
- Is there **complex multi-level approvals** (e.g., purchase requisition → PO → receipt → invoice)?

## 4. **Feature Scope**

- Which modules are **absolutely essential**? (e.g., Sales, Inventory, Finance, or also HR, Projects, CRM, Assets?)
- Do you need **multi-currency, multi-language, multi-tax**?
- Do you need **offline capability**? (e.g., sales reps on field with no internet?)
- Any **barcode/RFID/IoT integrations**?

## 5. **Integrations & Compliance**

- What **external systems** must it integrate with? (e.g., payment gateways, banks, SMS providers, third-party logistics, e-invoicing authorities, tax software?)
- Any **compliance requirements**? (e.g., GST/VAT, labor laws, data residency, GDPR?)
- Do you need **e-invoicing/e-way bills** (India-specific or region-specific)?

## 6. **Performance & Availability**

- Expected **uptime SLA**? (e.g., 99.5%, 99.9%?)
- Peak load scenario (e.g., "Black Friday 10x normal traffic")?
- Acceptable response time for critical operations? (e.g., <500ms for search, <2s for reporting?)

## 7. **Reporting & Analytics**

- Do you need **real-time dashboards** (P&L, sales pipeline, inventory aging)?
- How many **canned/ad-hoc reports**?
- **Data warehouse / BI tool** needed? (e.g., Power BI, Tableau, Looker?)

## 8. **Timeline & Constraints**

- When do you need **MVP (Minimum Viable Product)**? (weeks, months?)
- Any **existing legacy systems** we must migrate data from?
- Budget for **infrastructure** (self-hosted, cloud, hybrid)?

## 9. **Users & Roles**

- Typical **user roles**? (e.g., admin, manager, accountant, sales exec, warehouse staff, HR, CEO?)
- How many **admin vs end-users**?

## 10. **Data Sensitivity**

- Is **PII (Personally Identifiable Information) heavy**? (e.g., customer phone, employee SSN, bank details?)
- Need **encryption at rest** and **field-level masking**?

---

## 🚀 **If You Don't Have All Answers:**

I'll proceed with **reasonable assumptions** for a **typical medium-sized retail/distribution company** scenario:

### **Default Assumptions (if you skip the questions):**

✅ **CMR** = _Commerce & Retail Management system_  
✅ **Industry** = _Retail/Distribution with 3–5 branch locations_  
✅ **Scale** = _~200–500 concurrent users, ~5,000 daily transactions_  
✅ **Scope** = _Sales, Purchase, Inventory, Finance, CRM, HR, Reporting_  
✅ **Multi-tenant** = _No (single organization)_  
✅ **Geography** = _Single country (India), so GST + e-invoicing required_  
✅ **Key Integrations** = _Payment gateways, SMS, email, GST filing_  
✅ **Compliance** = _GST, labor laws, audit readiness_  
✅ **Uptime** = _99.5% SLA_  
✅ **Infrastructure** = _Cloud-native (AWS/Azure with PostgreSQL RDS)_  
✅ **Data Sensitivity** = _High (customer phone, bank details, employee SSN)_

---

## **Next Step:**

**Option A:** Reply with answers to the 10 questions above (I'll customize the design).  
**Option B:** Reply "**Use your default assumptions**" and I'll design for the retail/distribution scenario.  
**Option C:** Provide a **2-3 line brief** of your business and I'll ask 3–4 follow-ups.

---

**Which would you prefer?** Let me know, and I'll deliver a **complete, production-ready ERP blueprint** with:

- ✅ All modules + automation (ELI10)
- ✅ PostgreSQL schema
- ✅ REST APIs with DTOs
- ✅ Modular monolith .NET architecture
- ✅ Code examples
- ✅ Implementation roadmap

Here’s a much stronger, clearer prompt you can paste into ChatGPT to get a full ERP design (modules → DB → APIs → architecture) using .NET Web API + PostgreSQL + Identity + EF Core + Dapper, in a modular monolith Clean Architecture style.

---

## Improved Prompt (copy/paste)

You are a **world-class ERP system architect** and **principal .NET solution designer**.

### Context

Design a complete **ERP system for “CMR”** (define CMR as: _[insert what CMR stands for: company type, industry, size, locations, countries, number of users, online/offline needs]_).  
If any important detail is missing, **ask up to 10 clarifying questions first**, then proceed with reasonable assumptions and list them explicitly.

### Goals

I need an end-to-end ERP blueprint including:

1. **All ERP modules** (core + optional)
2. **Automation design** explained so a **10-year-old child can understand**
3. **Database schema (PostgreSQL)** with tables, keys, constraints, indexes
4. **API design** (RESTful .NET Web API) with endpoints + DTOs
5. **Modular Monolithic Clean Architecture** in .NET using:
    - ASP.NET Core Web API
    - PostgreSQL
    - ASP.NET Core Identity (RBAC)
    - EF Core (migrations + write model)
    - Dapper (read model / reporting)
    - Clear boundaries between modules (no tight coupling)

### Output Format (deliver in the order below)

#### Phase 1 — ERP Modules + Automation (ELI10 + professional view)

- Provide a **module list** grouped by domains (examples: Sales, Purchase, Inventory, Finance, HR, Projects, CRM, Asset, Manufacturing if applicable, Reporting).
- For each module include:
    - Purpose (1–2 lines)
    - Key features
    - Main entities (nouns)
    - Main workflows (verbs)
- **Automation**:
    - List top automation scenarios (e.g., “Auto reorder stock”, “Invoice generation”, “Approval workflows”, “Payroll scheduling”)
    - Explain each automation twice:
        1. **Like I’m 10 years old**
        2. Technical explanation (events, rules, triggers, background jobs)
- Include cross-cutting features:
    - Multi-tenant (if applicable)
    - Audit log
    - Notifications (email/SMS/in-app)
    - Attachments/documents
    - Localization/currency/timezone (if applicable)

#### Phase 2 — Domain Model + PostgreSQL Database Schema

- Provide:
    - High-level ERD description (text-based is fine)
    - Table-by-table schema with:
        - Columns + data types
        - PK/FK
        - Unique constraints
        - Indexes (including composite indexes where needed)
        - Soft delete strategy (if used)
        - Audit fields (CreatedAt, CreatedBy, etc.)
- Include:
    - Identity tables approach (ASP.NET Core Identity + custom user profile)
    - RBAC mapping (roles/permissions)
    - Tenant isolation strategy (if multi-tenant)
- Explain key tradeoffs (normalization vs reporting tables, etc.)

#### Phase 3 — API Design (ASP.NET Core Web API)

For each module:

- List REST endpoints with:
    - Method + route
    - Request DTO
    - Response DTO
    - Validation rules
    - Errors (standard format)
- Include:
    - Auth flow (Identity + JWT or cookie—choose and justify)
    - Authorization strategy (roles/permissions)
    - Pagination, filtering, sorting conventions
    - Idempotency for critical endpoints (e.g., payments)
    - API versioning strategy
    - Example: OpenAPI/Swagger structure outline

#### Phase 4 — Modular Monolith Clean Architecture (.NET)

Design a **modular monolith** with clear module boundaries.

- Provide solution structure, e.g.:
    - `/src/Api`
    - `/src/BuildingBlocks` (shared kernel)
    - `/src/Modules/Sales`
    - `/src/Modules/Inventory`
    - etc.
- For each module define:
    - Layers: Domain, Application, Infrastructure, API
    - Dependency rules (what can reference what)
    - How modules communicate (domain events, integration events, outbox pattern if needed)
- Data access pattern:
    - EF Core for writes (Aggregate roots, transactions)
    - Dapper for reads (query models, reporting views)
    - Explain when to use EF vs Dapper
- Cross-cutting:
    - Logging, tracing, exception handling middleware
    - Background jobs (Hangfire/Quartz—pick one)
    - Caching (optional)
    - Outbox/inbox pattern (if needed)
- Include minimal example code snippets:
    - One Aggregate + EF configuration
    - One command handler
    - One Dapper query
    - One controller endpoint

### Non-Functional Requirements (explicitly address)

- Performance targets (assume reasonable defaults if not provided)
